<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the directional zero–one conjecture for nearest-neighbor random walks in independent and identically distributed strictly elliptic environments on ℤ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, <i>d</i> ≥ 3: the probability of escape in each fixed nonzero real direction is zero or one. Only strict positivity of the transition probabilities is required; no uniform lower bound or moment assumption is imposed.

- **claim-directional-zero-one** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $d\ge3$, let $\mathbb P$ be an independent and identically distributed law of nearest-neighbor transition rows on $\mathbb Z^d$ satisfying <a href="#eq:ellipticity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ellipticity">[eq:ellipticity]</a>, and let $\ell\in\mathbb R^d\setminus\{0\}$. Then $$P_0(A_\ell)\in\{0,1\}.$$
  - Explanation: For nearest-neighbor walks in iid strictly elliptic environments on the lattice in dimension at least three, the annealed probability of escaping in any fixed nonzero real direction is either zero or one. No uniform lower bound on transition probabilities is needed.
- **claim-departure-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-departure-transfer)): Let $\gamma=(x_0,\ldots,x_m)$ be a finite nearest-neighbor word and let $f(\omega)$ be a nonnegative measurable function of the rows outside $\operatorname{Dep}(\gamma)$. Then $$\label{eq:departure-transfer} \mathbb E\!\left[ \prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\omega(x_j,x_{j+1}-x_j)f(\omega)\right] =W(\gamma)\mathbb E[f].$$ In particular, two prescribed words with disjoint departure sets have the same joint weight when sampled under independent annealed laws as when sampled independently conditional on one shared environment.* *The identity also applies when $f(\omega)$ is the quenched probability of an event for another walk stopped on its first arrival in $\operatorname{Dep}(\gamma)$. Events that require this other walk to avoid that set forever are allowed.
  - Explanation: A path weight depends only on the rows at its departure sites. A nonnegative function of the other rows therefore factors out of the expectation, and two words with disjoint departure sets can be sampled independently or in a shared environment with the same joint weight.
- **claim-finite-height-supremum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-supremum)): For this fixed real direction, almost surely under $P_0$, $$\sup_{n\ge0}h(X_n)<\infty \quad\Longrightarrow\quad h(X_n)\longrightarrow-\infty.$$ In particular, a finite height interval cannot contain the path forever. The corresponding statements hold from every translated starting site, and hold quenched for almost every environment, simultaneously for all such starting sites.
  - Explanation: If the projected height of the walk has a finite supremum, then it tends to minus infinity, so no finite height interval can trap the path forever. The same holds from every starting site and in the quenched sense.
- **claim-positive-nonbacktracking** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-nonbacktracking)): If $P_0(A_v)>0$, then $p_v>0$.
  - Explanation: Positive probability of escape in a direction forces positive probability that the walk never drops below its starting height in that direction.
- **claim-regeneration-slabs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slabs)): Suppose $p_v>0$. Under $\widehat P_v$, there are almost surely infinitely many true cuts $0=\tau_0<\tau_1<\tau_2<\cdots$, with time zero included as an initial boundary. The relative words between consecutive cuts are independent and identically distributed, with probability law $\nu_v$ given by $$\nu_v(\gamma)=W(\gamma)$$ on the regeneration words in direction $v$. The departure heights within each word belong to $[0,L)$ relative to its starting height.* *Under the unconditioned law, on $\{\sup_n h(X_n)=\infty\}$ a first true strict record exists almost surely. Conditional on its finite prefix, the translated future has law $\widehat P_v$.
  - Explanation: Under the nonbacktracking conditioning there are infinitely many true cuts, and the relative words between consecutive cuts are iid with law given by the annealed path weight on regeneration words.
- **claim-mean-width** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mean-width)): Suppose $p_v>0$ and $\\|v\\|_\infty=1$. If $S$ is the number of ordinary strict records after the initial boundary of a slab with law $\nu_v$, including its terminal record, then $$0<\mathbb E_{\nu_v}L\le\mathbb E_{\nu_v}S\le p_v^{-1}<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A slab has positive and finite mean projected width, bounded by the mean number of ordinary records, which is bounded by the inverse of the nonbacktracking probability.
- **claim-sign-union** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sign-union)): For every fixed nonzero real direction $v$, if $P_0(A_v)>0$, then $$P_0(A_v\cup A_{-v})=1.$$ More precisely, up to null sets, infinite height supremum implies $A_v$ and finite height supremum implies $A_{-v}$.
  - Explanation: If escape in a direction has positive probability, then the walk escapes in that direction or its opposite almost surely: infinite height supremum gives escape forward, finite height supremum gives escape backward.
- **claim-spatial-radius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-radius)): Let the environment consist of iid strictly positive transition rows, and let $v\in\mathbb R^d\setminus\{0\}$. Suppose $P_0(A_v)>0$ and $P_0(A_{-v})>0$. For each sign $\sigma\in\{+,-\}$, let $\nu_{\sigma v}$ be the slab law from Lemma <a href="#lem:slabs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:slabs">5</a>. If $R$ is the maximum Euclidean distance from a slab’s initial site, including its terminal site, then $$\int R\,d\nu_v+\int R\,d\nu_{-v}<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: When both signs of a direction have positive escape probability, the spatial radius of a slab has finite mean under both slab laws. The argument turns a huge excursion into a tilted record from which a fresh opposite continuation keeps the height maximum finite.
- **claim-initial-averages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-initial-averages)): Let $(Z_j)_{j\ge1}$ be a stationary sequence of nonnegative integrable random variables. For every $a>0$ and positive integer $n$, $$P\left(\max_{1\le k\le n}\frac1k\sum_{j=1}^kZ_j>a\right) \le \frac{EZ_1}{a}.$$
  - Explanation: A maximal inequality for a stationary nonnegative integrable sequence: the chance that some initial average exceeds a level is at most the mean divided by that level.
- **claim-coordinate-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-coordinate-reduction)): Suppose that the annealed walk satisfies $0<P_0(A_v)<1$ for some $v\in\mathbb R^d\setminus\{0\}$. There is a coordinate $i$ for which $$P_0(A_{e_i})>0\qquad\text{and}\qquad P_0(A_{-e_i})>0.$$
  - Explanation: If escape in some real direction has probability strictly between zero and one, then both signs of some coordinate direction have positive escape probability. The proof builds two limiting rays and shows they are opposite.
- **claim-exact-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-bridge)): For either sign, the probability under $\widehat P_\sigma$ of a slab cut at height $H\ge0$ is $u_H$. The numbers $u_H$ satisfy $$u_0=1,\qquad 1\ge u_H\ge u_{H+1}\ge p_\sigma.$$ Conditional on a cut at $H$, the list of slabs through that cut has law $\mathcal B_H^\sigma$ given by $$\label{eq:exact-bridge-list} \mathcal B_H^\sigma(\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_m) =\frac{1}{u_H^\sigma}\prod_{i=1}^m W(\gamma_i), \qquad \sum_{i=1}^m L(\gamma_i)=H.$$ Concatenation gives the raw path stopped on its first hit of $H$, conditional on reaching $H$ before $-1$, in the signed height. Reversing the order of the list preserves $\mathcal B_H^\sigma$; every word is still traversed in its original chronological order.
  - Explanation: The probability of a slab cut at a given height is a nonincreasing sequence bounded below by the nonbacktracking probability, and the slab list through that cut has a normalized product law invariant under list reversal.
- **claim-renewal-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-renewal-tail)): For either sign and all integers $n'\ge n\ge0$, $$\label{eq:renewal-tail} 0\le u_n-u_{n'}\le(n'-n)F(n).$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:dyadic-width-tail} \sum_{l\ge0}2^lF(2^l)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Differences of cut probabilities are controlled by the slab-width tail, and the dyadic weighted sum of that tail is finite.
- **claim-common-blocks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-common-blocks)): The paired lists in successive common blocks are iid. If $K$ is the total width of one block, then $$\label{eq:common-width-moment} \mathbf E K\le\frac{1}{p_+p_-}<\infty.$$ Let $V_+$ and $V_-$ be the sums of the actual chronological lateral displacements of the positive and negative words in that block. Then $$\label{eq:common-lateral-moment} \mathbf E(\|V_+\|+\|V_-\|)<\infty.$$ When passing down through this block, the negative-minus-positive lateral gap increases by $$S=V_++V_-\in\mathbb Z^{d-1},\qquad \mathbf E\|S\|<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: The two opposed tapes decompose into iid common blocks with finite mean width and finite mean lateral gap increment.
- **claim-entropy-upper-contacts** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-entropy-upper)): Suppose that both coordinate signs have positive directional-transience probability. In the opposed arrangement of Section <a href="#sec:arrangement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arrangement">4</a>, let $m(J)$ be the expected number of dyadic common-block index intervals $(2^j,2^{j+1}]$, $1\le j\le J$, containing a departure-site contact, as in <a href="#eq:contact-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contact-count">[eq:contact-count]</a>. Then $$m(J)=O\!\left(\frac{J}{\log J}\right)\qquad(J\longrightarrow\infty).$$
  - Explanation: With both signs transient, the expected number of dyadic block-index intervals containing a contact grows at most like the scale divided by its logarithm.
- **claim-entropy-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-comparison)): There is a constant $C$, independent of $n$, such that $$D(P_n\Vert Q_n)\le C+h_{14n}-h_n.$$
  - Explanation: The relative entropy between the actual law and the independent-bridge reference law is bounded by a constant plus an increment of displacement entropy.
- **claim-reference-contact** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-contact)): For the reference experiment just defined, $$Q_n(E_{\mathrm{mid}}) \le\delta_n:=\min\{1,Cn(F_+(n)+F_-(n))\}.$$
  - Explanation: Under the reference law a middle contact is rare, with probability bounded through the slab-width tails.
- **claim-posterior-maxima** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-posterior-maxima)): Let $m\ge1$, and let $(w_i(e))_{i\ge0}$, $1\le e\le m$, be nonnegative martingales for a filtration $(\mathcal F_i)$, with $\sum_{e=1}^m w_i(e)\le1$ almost surely. Define $$A_i(e)=\max_{0\le j\le i}w_j(e),\qquad A_i=\sum_{e=1}^m A_i(e).$$ Write $A_\infty(e)=\lim_{i\to\infty}A_i(e)$ and $A_\infty=\sum_e A_\infty(e)=\lim_{i\to\infty}A_i$. There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ such that $$\label{eq:posterior-exponential} \mathbb E\exp\left(\frac{cA_\infty}{\log(m+1)}\right)\le C.$$ Moreover, if $V$ is any event on a joint probability space with this martingale-path marginal and $p=P(V)$, then $$\label{eq:posterior-event-weight} \mathbb E[A_\infty\mathbf 1_V] \le C\log(m+1)\,p\log(e/p),$$ where the right side is zero when $p=0$. The event $V$ need not belong to any $\mathcal F_i$.
  - Explanation: For finitely many nonnegative martingales with total mass at most one, the sum of running maxima has an exponential tail on a logarithmic scale, and a weighted bound holds on any event.
- **claim-first-contact-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-contact-bound)): In the preceding experiment, let $0<k<r<N$ be integers and define $$\tau=\inf\{i\ge T_k:i<T_N,\ Y_i\in\{\beta_j:j\ge0\}\}, \qquad \Delta=u^+_{r-k}-u^+_{N-k}.$$ An empty infimum is $+\infty$. Then $$\begin{gathered} \label{eq:first-contact-bound} P\left(T_r\le\tau<T_N,\ \min_{T_k\le i\le\tau}(Y_i)_1\ge k,\ \\|E\\|\le N^2\right)\\ \le C\log(2N)\,\Delta\log^2(e/\Delta), \end{gathered}$$ with right side zero when $\Delta=0$.
  - Explanation: When the negative path starts at the random endpoint of the positive bridge, the probability of a first contact after a prescribed height is bounded using the cut-probability increment and the logarithm of the endpoint set size.
- **claim-contact-lower** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-contact-lower)): Suppose that both coordinate signs have positive directional-transience probability. In the opposed arrangement of Section <a href="#sec:arrangement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arrangement">4</a>, let $m(J)$ be the expected number of dyadic common-block index intervals $(2^j,2^{j+1}]$, $1\le j\le J$, containing a departure-site contact, as in <a href="#eq:contact-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contact-count">[eq:contact-count]</a>. There are constants $c>0$ and $J_0<\infty$ such that $$m(J)\ge \frac{cJ}{\log\log(3+J)}\qquad(J\ge J_0).$$
  - Explanation: With both signs transient, the expected number of contact intervals has a lower bound growing like the scale divided by an iterated logarithm, which is incompatible with the upper bound.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "For either sign, the probability under $\\widehat P_\\sigma$ of a slab cut at height $H\\ge0$ is $u_H$. The numbers $u_H$ satisfy $$u_0=1,\\qquad 1\\ge u_H\\ge u_{H+1}\\ge p_\\sigma.$$ Conditional on a cut at $H$, the list of slabs through that cut has law $\\mathcal B_H^\\sigma$ given by $$\\label{eq:exact-bridge-list}\n \\mathcal B_H^\\sigma(\\gamma_1,\\ldots,\\gamma_m)\n =\\frac{1}{u_H^\\sigma}\\prod_{i=1}^m W(\\gamma_i),\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m L(\\gamma_i)=H.$$ Concatenation gives the raw path stopped on its first hit of $H$, conditional on reaching $H$ before $-1$, in the signed height. Reversing the order of the list preserves $\\mathcal B_H^\\sigma$; every word is still traversed in its original chronological order.",
        "source_id": "PM-220.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-common-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "The paired lists in successive common blocks are iid. If $K$ is the total width of one block, then $$\\label{eq:common-width-moment}\n \\mathbf E K\\le\\frac{1}{p_+p_-}<\\infty.$$ Let $V_+$ and $V_-$ be the sums of the actual chronological lateral displacements of the positive and negative words in that block. Then $$\\label{eq:common-lateral-moment}\n \\mathbf E(|V_+|+|V_-|)<\\infty.$$ When passing down through this block, the negative-minus-positive lateral gap increases by $$S=V_++V_-\\in\\mathbb Z^{d-1},\\qquad \\mathbf E|S|<\\infty.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-220.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $m\\ge1$, and let $(w_i(e))_{i\\ge0}$, $1\\le e\\le m$, be nonnegative martingales for a filtration $(\\mathcal F_i)$, with $\\sum_{e=1}^m w_i(e)\\le1$ almost surely. Define $$A_i(e)=\\max_{0\\le j\\le i}w_j(e),\\qquad\n A_i=\\sum_{e=1}^m A_i(e).$$ Write $A_\\infty(e)=\\lim_{i\\to\\infty}A_i(e)$ and $A_\\infty=\\sum_e A_\\infty(e)=\\lim_{i\\to\\infty}A_i$. There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:posterior-exponential}\n \\mathbb E\\exp\\left(\\frac{cA_\\infty}{\\log(m+1)}\\right)\\le C.$$ Moreover, if $V$ is any event on a joint probability space with this martingale-path marginal and $p=P(V)$, then $$\\label{eq:posterior-event-weight}\n \\mathbb E[A_\\infty\\mathbf 1_V]\n \\le C\\log(m+1)\\,p\\log(e/p),$$ where the right side is zero when $p=0$. The event $V$ need not belong to any $\\mathcal F_i$.",

      },

There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:posterior-exponential}\n \\mathbb E\\exp\\left(\\frac{cA_\\infty}{\\log(m+1)}\\right)\\le C.$$ Moreover, if $V$ is any event on a joint probability space with this martingale-path marginal and $p=P(V)$, then $$\\label{eq:posterior-event-weight}\n \\mathbb E[A_\\infty\\mathbf 1_V]\n \\le C\\log(m+1)\\,p\\log(e/p),$$ where the right side is zero when $p=0$.       "explanation": "With both signs transient, the expected number of contact intervals has a lower bound growing like the scale divided by an iterated logarithm, which is incompatible with the upper bound.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-contact-lower",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that both coordinate signs have positive directional-transience probability.",
          "source_id": "PM-220.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-contact-lower",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [

          "source_id": "PM-220.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-contact-lower"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",

  "schema": "pm.claims.v2"
}

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **row** ([source](#eq:ellipticity)): We call this vector the *row* at $x$.
- **strict ellipticity** ([source](#eq:ellipticity)): We call this *strict ellipticity*.
- **finite nearest-neighbor word** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A finite nearest-neighbor word is a sequence $\gamma=(x_0,\ldots,x_m)$ with $x_{j+1}-x_j\in U$.
- **contact** ([source](#pm-h-19)): A *contact* is a site that is a departure of both placed tapes.

| q-mean-width-bound | expected number of ordinary records in a slab | ≤ | $p_v^{-1}$ | Direction normalized by the supremum norm. |

| q-renewal-difference | difference of cut probabilities | ≤ | $(n'-n)F(n)$ | Integers n' at least n at least zero. |
| q-common-width-mean | expected width of a common block | ≤ | $\frac{1}{p_+p_-}$ | First common block under the joint law. |

| q-contact-count-upper | expected number of contact intervals | = | $O\!\left(\frac{J}{\log J}\right)$ | Asymptotic as J tends to infinity. |
| q-contact-count-lower | expected number of contact intervals | ≥ | $\frac{cJ}{\log\log(3+J)}$ | All J at least J_0. |
| q-posterior-exponential-moment | exponential moment of the sum of posterior maxima | ≤ | $C$ | Scale of the logarithm of m plus one. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-departure-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-departure-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-departure-transfer): Factor the product over departure rows from the function of other rows using independence; extend to stopped walks by a monotone class argument. -> transfer identity for path weights
- **step-finite-supremum** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-supremum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-supremum): Repeated scripted trials at recurrent sites or at fresh columns, with the strong Markov property and fresh rows, rule out confinement. -> finite supremum forces escape to minus infinity
- **step-positive-nonbacktracking** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-nonbacktracking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-nonbacktracking): Cover escape by countably many global-minimum events and use translation invariance. -> positive nonbacktracking probability
- **step-regeneration-slabs** [claim](#stmt-lem-slabs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slabs): Stopping-time trials give existence of true cuts; exact factorization of prefix weight and suffix law gives iid slabs. -> iid slab sequence
- **step-mean-width** [claim](#stmt-lem-mean-width) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mean-width): Renewal counting of record indices with the strong law and dominated convergence. -> finite positive mean width
- **step-sign-union** [claim](#stmt-lem-sign-union) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sign-union): Split on infinite or finite height supremum and apply slab and finite supremum lemmas. -> escape in one of two opposite signs almost surely
- **step-initial-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-initial-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-averages): Disjoint witness intervals cover bad starts; take expectations with stationarity. -> maximal inequality for initial averages
- **step-spatial-radius** [claim](#stmt-prop-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-averages-2): Assume infinite truncated radius mean, choose scales, find a large slab after controlled prefix, create a tilted record, continue with a fresh opposite path, and contradict the tail of finite maxima. -> finite mean spatial radius
- **step-coordinate-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-coordinate-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coordinate-reduction): Strong law gives limiting rays; a tail zero-one argument shows the rays are opposite; pick a coordinate. -> coordinate direction with both signs positive
- **step-exact-bridge** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-bridge): Unique slab decomposition of paths hitting a height before minus one, with product weights. -> bridge law and cut probabilities
- **step-renewal-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-renewal-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-renewal-tail): Renewal identity over the last cut, subtract consecutive identities, telescope, and group dyadically. -> tail comparison
- **step-common-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lem-common-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-common-blocks): Condition on word counts to get renewal at common cuts, derive a renewal identity for width, and use Tonelli for lateral moments. -> iid common blocks with finite moments
- **step-entropy-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-comparison): Bound conditional relative entropy by the entropy of chunk heights, then handle the alignment variable through entropy of buffer sums. -> relative entropy bound
- **step-reference-contact** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-contact): Transfer to a shared environment, classify contact sites by quenched exit probabilities, and apply the renewal tail. -> small reference contact probability
- **step-entropy-upper** [claim](#stmt-prop-entropy-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-contact-2): Binary relative entropy inequality at each dyadic scale, summed with telescoping entropy increments. -> upper bound for contact count
- **step-posterior-maxima** [claim](#stmt-lem-posterior-maxima) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-posterior-maxima): Bound conditional future increases by entropy, use threshold crossings for an exponential tail, then conditional Jensen for events. -> exponential and event-weight bounds
- **step-first-contact-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-contact-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-contact-bound): Enumerate lower prefixes, transfer to a shared environment, use stopped posterior maxima and dyadic bins of quenched exit probability. -> first-contact bound
- **step-contact-lower** [claim](#stmt-prop-contact-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-contact-lower): Condition on a cut at large depth, force late first contacts at uncovered scales, average over cuts, and convert depth scales to block indices. -> lower bound for contact count
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-contact-lower-2): Assume an intermediate probability, reduce to a coordinate direction, and contradict the upper and lower contact bounds. -> zero-one law

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-directional-zero-one**: For an iid strictly elliptic nearest-neighbor environment on the lattice in dimension at least three, the annealed probability of escape in any fixed nonzero real direction is either zero or one.
- **inv-sign-union**: If escape in a fixed nonzero real direction has positive probability, then escape in that direction or in its opposite occurs almost surely.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-strict-rows**: Apply the zero-one law to a walk where some transition probability may vanish. -> The proof uses positivity of every transition entry to build fresh-row scripts, so the conclusion is not covered.
- **anti-low-dimension**: Use the theorem for a planar or one-dimensional walk. -> The theorem is stated only for dimension at least three; the argument is not claimed there.
- **anti-dependent-environment**: Apply the result to a stationary but dependent environment. -> Independence of rows is used substantively; dependent environments can have opposite escape with positive probability.
- **anti-speed-claim**: Read the theorem as giving a positive speed or ballisticity. -> No speed or duration moment is obtained, so such conclusions are unsupported.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a probabilistic existence statement proved by contradiction between two asymptotic bounds on the contact count; it names no finite procedure that outputs an object, so there is nothing finite to execute or test with an oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a probabilistic zero-one law for random walks in random environments; it states no procedure or interface a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns infinite-horizon escape of a walk, which no finite simulation can decide. Simulations could only illustrate it, and the paper offers no sampling procedure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or decision procedure is produced; the argument is a proof by contradiction between two asymptotic contact-count bounds. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is non-constructive, using strong laws, tail triviality and entropy inequalities, and gives no algorithm with stated complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The regeneration slabs, tapes and common blocks are proof devices for analysing a random path, not data structures with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardware, parallelism or circuits. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption or security notion appears in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The posterior-martingale maximal inequality is an internal probabilistic tool for endpoint posteriors of a walk. It is not a learning guarantee and the paper has no model or training procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective, constraint set or optimisation problem; the only extremal statements are probability bounds inside the contradiction. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lattice walk is a mathematical model; the paper makes no claim about networks, messages, consensus or failures. Mapping it to a mesh needs independent transition rules and strict positivity, which the paper does not address. |
| other (probability_theory_random_walks_in_random_environment) | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The result belongs to probability theory: it settles the directional zero-one law for random walks in iid strictly elliptic environments, and the regeneration and contact machinery is reusable proof technique for related walk models. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-strict-ellipticity** (hypothesis): The conclusion is proved only when every nearest-neighbor transition probability is almost surely positive. This is the only quantitative assumption on the rows, so a model with a vanishing transition probability is not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the zero-one law to a walk where some step has zero probability.']
- **limit-dimension** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated for dimension at least three. The planar case is treated by different arguments in the literature and is not claimed here. Blocked use: ['Citing the theorem for a planar or one-dimensional walk.']
- **limit-iid-rows** (hypothesis): Independence of rows across sites is used substantively; dependent stationary environments can have positive probability of escape in opposite directions. Blocked use: ['Applying the result to a stationary but dependent environment without independence.']
- **limit-no-speed** (formal_scope): The theorem asserts only that the escape probability in a fixed direction is zero or one. No speed, ballisticity or moment of a regeneration duration is used or obtained. Blocked use: ['Reading the theorem as a positive speed or ballisticity statement.']
- **limit-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The theorem is proved by contradiction from an assumed intermediate probability, using two incompatible asymptotic contact-count bounds with unspecified constants. It yields no procedure and no explicit constants. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or explicit numerical bound from the proof.']
- **limit-radius-coexistence** (hypothesis): The finite mean spatial radius of a slab is derived only under the hypothesis that both signs of the direction have positive escape probability. Blocked use: ['Using the radius moment bound for a walk with escape in only one sign.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 220 (`lean/docs/220.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A directional zero–one law under strict ellipticity](../../preprints/A-directional-zero-one-law-under-strict-ellipticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Directional transience implies ballisticity](../../preprints/Directional-transience-implies-ballisticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The directional zero–one conjecture asks whether a random walk's probability of escape in a fixed direction must be zero or one. The formalization proves this for nearest-neighbor walks in independent identically distributed strictly elliptic environments on $\mathbb Z^d$, for every $d\ge3$ and every nonzero real direction. Strict ellipticity means that every allowed transition probability is positive almost surely; no uniform positive lower bound or moment condition is assumed. The probability is the annealed law from the origin.
>
> For an i.i.d. uniformly elliptic nearest-neighbor random environment on $\mathbb Z^d$, the formalization proves that almost-sure directional transience implies convergence of $X_n/n$ to a deterministic velocity with positive projection in that direction for every $d\ge2$.
>
> For $d\ge3$, positive probability of transience in any nonzero direction already suffices. The limiting velocity is unique, and the unit directions with positive transience probability are exactly the open hemisphere having positive inner product with that velocity; transience has probability one on that hemisphere and zero on its complement. The environment is sampled once and retained along the walk, and the probabilities use the annealed law from the origin.

Comparator source `DirectionalWalk.lean` (3388 bytes, sha256 `8ad4588537c02fac9d123a9ba7fd780e9eda1575b48f674320733fad3938da11`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter Preorder
open scoped ENNReal BigOperators Topology

namespace DirectionalZeroOne

abbrev Site (d : ℕ) := Fin d → ℤ
abbrev Step (d : ℕ) := Fin d × Bool

def stepVector {d : ℕ} (e : Step d) : Site d :=
  fun i => if i = e.1 then (if e.2 then 1 else -1) else 0

def Row (d : ℕ) :=
  {p : Step d → ℝ // (∀ e, p e ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) ∧ ∑ e, p e = 1}

instance (d : ℕ) : MeasurableSpace (Row d) := inferInstanceAs
  (MeasurableSpace {p : Step d → ℝ //
    (∀ e, p e ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) ∧ ∑ e, p e = 1})

abbrev Environment (d : ℕ) := Site d → Row d
abbrev Path (d : ℕ) := ℕ → Site d
abbrev State (d : ℕ) := Environment d × Site d

def StrictEllipticity {d : ℕ} (μ : Measure (Row d)) : Prop :=
  ∀ᵐ p ∂μ, ∀ e, 0 < p.val e

noncomputable def environmentLaw {d : ℕ} (μ : Measure (Row d))
    [IsProbabilityMeasure μ] : Measure (Environment d) :=
  Measure.infinitePi (fun _ => μ)

noncomputable def transition (d : ℕ) : Kernel (State d) (State d) where
  toFun s := ∑ e : Step d,
    ENNReal.ofReal ((s.1 s.2).val e) • Measure.dirac (s.1, s.2 + stepVector e)
  measurable' := by
    apply measurable_from_prod_countable_left
    intro x
    apply Finset.measurable_sum
    intro e he
    apply Measure.measurable_of_measurable_coe
    intro t ht
    simp only [Measure.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul]
    apply Measurable.mul
    · exact (measurable_pi_apply e |>.comp
        (measurable_subtype_coe.comp (measurable_pi_apply x))).ennreal_ofReal
    · exact (Measure.measurable_coe ht).comp
        (Measure.measurable_dirac.comp (measurable_id.prodMk measurable_const))

instance (d : ℕ) : IsMarkovKernel (transition d) where
  isProbabilityMeasure s := by
    constructor
    change (∑ e : Step d,
      ENNReal.ofReal ((s.1 s.2).val e) •
        Measure.dirac (s.1, s.2 + stepVector e)) Set.univ = 1
    simp only [Measure.finsetSum_apply, Measure.smul_apply,
      Measure.dirac_apply_of_mem (Set.mem_univ _), smul_eq_mul, mul_one]
    rw [← ENNReal.ofReal_sum_of_nonneg]
    · rw [(s.1 s.2).property.2, ENNReal.ofReal_one]
    · intro e he
      exact ((s.1 s.2).property.1 e).1

noncomputable def historyKernel (d n : ℕ) :
    Kernel ((i : Finset.Iic n) → State d) (State d) :=
  (transition d).comap (fun h => h ⟨n, Finset.mem_Iic.mpr le_rfl⟩)
    (measurable_pi_apply _)

instance (d n : ℕ) : IsMarkovKernel (historyKernel d n) := by
  unfold historyKernel
  infer_instance

noncomputable def initialLaw {d : ℕ} (μ : Measure (Row d))
    [IsProbabilityMeasure μ] (x : Site d) : Measure (State d) :=
  (environmentLaw μ).map (fun ω => (ω, x))

noncomputable def annealed {d : ℕ} (μ : Measure (Row d))
    [IsProbabilityMeasure μ] (x : Site d) : Measure (Path d) :=
  (Kernel.trajMeasure (X := fun _ => State d) (initialLaw μ x) (historyKernel d)).map
    (fun z n => (z n).2)

def escape {d : ℕ} (ℓ : Fin d → ℝ) : Set (Path d) :=
  {X | Tendsto (fun n => ∑ i, (X n i : ℝ) * ℓ i) atTop atTop}

theorem directional_zero_one (d : ℕ) (hd : 3 ≤ d)
    (μ : Measure (Row d)) [IsProbabilityMeasure μ]
    (hell : StrictEllipticity μ) (ℓ : Fin d → ℝ) (hℓ : ℓ ≠ 0) :
    annealed μ 0 (escape ℓ) = 0 ∨ annealed μ 0 (escape ℓ) = 1 := by sorry

end DirectionalZeroOne

end OAI
```

The limiting velocity is unique, and the unit directions with positive transience probability are exactly the open hemisphere having positive inner product with that velocity; transience has probability one on that hemisphere and zero on its complement. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the directional zero–one conjecture for nearest-neighbor random walks in independent and identically distributed strictly elliptic environments on ℤ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, <i>d</i> ≥ 3: the probability of escape in each fixed nonzero real direction is zero or one. Only strict positivity of the transition probabilities is required; no uniform lower bound or moment assumption is imposed.
